EXHIBIT L
TENANT IMPROVEMENT ALLOWANCE
Landlord will pay Tenant an amount of $410,037.50 for Tenant’s Work. Collectively, the same
will be referred to as the “Tenant Improvement Allowance”.
The Tenant Improvement Allowance shall be paid one-half to Tenant at the time that
Tenant’s Work is approximately fifty percent (50%) complete. Tenant’s Work will be deemed to
be fifty percent (50%) complete at such time as (A) so certified in writing by Tenant’s architect
(as reasonably approved by Landlord), (B) Landlord has received copies of executed general
contractor and subcontractor interim lien waivers, and (C) Landlord has received copies of paid
invoices for the work for which reimbursement is
sought. The balance of the Tenant
Improvement Allowance will be paid to Tenant within thirty (30) days after satisfaction of all of
the following: (i) completion of the Tenant’s Work in accordance with the approved plans and
specifications and the provisions hereof; (ii) delivery of an affidavit that all payrolls, bills for
materials and any equipment and other indebtedness connected with Tenant’s Work have been
paid or otherwise satisfied, including general contractor and subcontractor lien waivers; (iii) and
delivery of all certificates necessary for occupancy of the Premises issued by the appropriate
governmental authority permitting use of the Premises in accordance with the approved plans
and specifications; and (iv) Tenant’s opening for business in the Premises.
In the event that Landlord fails to pay the Tenant Improvement Allowance to Tenant
within thirty (30) days described above, and such failure continues for a period of ten (10) days
after written notice thereof to Landlord, Tenant shall thereafter have the right to offset the amount
of the Tenant Improvement Allowance against any amount owed by Tenant to Landlord.
00768-078/00290624-8

Alcohol Training and Compliance Program
Vitamin Cottage Natural Food Markets, Inc.
As an experienced retailer of alcoholic beverages in eight states, Vitamin Cottage Natural Food Markets,
Inc. (“Natural Grocers”) has developed a rigorous training and compliance program for employees and
managers at stores where alcohol is sold. For each location where alcohol is sold, this program is carefully
tailored to ensure it incorporates all state and local requirements, and is vetted by Natural Grocers’ legal
and operations teams.
Should Natural Grocers be issued a Retail Liquor License for its Cheyenne store, Natural Grocers is
committed to working with law enforcement and other governmental authorities to ensure that our program
meets all state and local requirements. This program would include, at minimum, orientation training for
all persons selling alcohol from our Cheyenne store, as well as any personnel providing security at the
premises, which training would be improved in advance by the Wyoming Liquor Division. All such
personnel would be require to complete the orientation within ninety (90) days of employment, as well as
any continuing training required by state and local authorities. Natural Grocers’ training program for its
Cheyenne store will include direction to employees on how to verify that a customer is of legal age to
purchase alcohol, how to report incidents on the premises requiring law enforcement response services, and
any other information recommended by applicable authorities.
To demonstrate the scope and level of detail of the Natural Grocers alcohol training program, enclosed
please a printout of our current training program and procedures for select markets. Should Natural Grocersr
receive a Retail Liquor License for its Cheyenne store, this document and its online counterpart will be
updated to include all state and local requirements.

1.3 Select State
Introduction
This training is intended for all Crew at Natural Grocers stores that have a Cottage
alcoholic beverage store or department. We have high quality standards for
Cottage products, and it’s important that you can communicate to our customers
about what makes them unique. Additionally, Cottage products have special
receiving and selling regulations compared to other products in your store that
must be followed to maintain compliance with the law.
Throughout this training, you will learn
• The products you will find in the Cottage part of your store
• How alcohol-containing products are received and sold
• Company policies and legal regulations governing store operations

2.4 Damaged Product
• When exposed to air, alcohol will oxidize, creating a strong smell
that will attract insects. So, do not keep opened or leaking alcohol
products in the store.
If the bottle is leaking or slightly broken, pour out contents
of the container into the mop sink and rinse it out.
If a bottle is shattered or broken, be careful to
not cut yourself. Dispose of the glass inside a
cardboard box. Label the box “broken glass” and
tape it shut. Throw it away in the outside dumpster.
Continue
Notes:
2.5EAS
Theft Deterrence
Your store may have bottle
toppers which are hard
plastic devices that fit over
the top of bottle necks to
help deter theft.
These tags are reusable and are physically removed from
the product during checkout. A bottle topper remover will be
available at one of your store’s registers and will be used to
remove bottle toppers after a customer has purchased the
capped product.

2.6 Coverage Requirements
Crew Coverage Requirements
•
As an issue of legal compliance, there must always be a qualified Crew
member in The Cottage Wine and Craft Beer store at all times.
If there is only one Crew member in The Cottage, that Crew member may
not leave the department to provide register backup on the main store
registers.
Even if there are no customers in The Cottage, the Crew member assigned
to The Cottage cannot leave to assist in other departments unless there is
double coverage within the Cottage.
If for any reason The Cottage must be left unattended, all doors must be
locked before leaving and must remain secured until coverage is returned.
Notes:
Published by Articulate® Storyline www.articulate.com

2.7 Coverage Requirements
Coverage Requirements
If a sole Crew member providing coverage to the Cottage Wine and
Craft Beer store needs to leave to take a lunch break or use the
restroom, another qualified Crew member must provide coverage
for the department during this time.
A qualified Crew member is:
1,
21 years of aye or olderAND
2.
Currently certified as having successfully completed the
ServSafe Alcohol training program or equivalent.

4.5 Refusing a Sale
Refusing a Sale
You must refuse an alcohol sale when the
customer Is unable to provide proof of legal age
(the ID appears fake, does not match the
individual, has expired, or the person has no ID).
It is also your legal duty to refuse sale in the
following situations:
• The person is apparently attempting to
purchase alcohol for anyone under 21.
• The person appears intoxicated (shows two
or more signs of Intoxication).
o
Common signs include swaying while
standing, glassy eyes, and smelling like
alcohol.
You can find a more complete list of
signs linked in the course Resources.

•
Certification training and exams must be completed during a
scheduled shift. Missing a scheduled training will result in

• Your store may occasionally have in-store demos/tastings for

followed whenever a tasting is occurring at your store.

full list of instructions for conducting a successful tasting including
how to set it up, write-off product, engage with customers, and clean
up after the demo.

5.3 NonReceiving
Receiving Cottage Products
Please let your Receiving Manager, Assistant Store Manager or Store
Manager know if there is a delivery for alcoholic product.
• It is a legal requirement that someone at the store sign that they have received
and taken control of the alcohol that has been delivered.
If the Receiving Manager is not available to receive the delivery, you MUST
page an MOD trained on receiving procedures to receive the alcohol delivery
and sign for it.
• If you are not trained on receiving procedures, DO NOT sign the invoice. By
signing you are confirming that the delivery is correct and complete, and you
are committing the receiving entity to pay for the full amount of the invoice.
5.4 Receiving
Receiving Cottage Products
It is a legal requirement that someone at the store sign that they
have received and taken control of the alcohol as soon as it is
delivered.
If the Receiving Manager is not available to receive the delivery, any
other Crew membertrained on receiving procedures and access to
SAP must receive the alcohol delivery and sign for it.
By signing the invoice, the Crew member is confirming that the
delivery is correct and complete, and they are committing the
receiving entity to pay for the full amount of the invoice.
The signature of the person receiving the alcohol is mandated by
state statute. Failure to comply will result in disciplinary action.
Published by Articulate® Storyline www.articulate.com

DT Receiving (Slide Layer)
Receiving Cottage Products
• Product containing alcohol must be received by Crew who is
18 or older.
• Once product is received, it must be taken to Cottage Wine and Craft
Beer immediately by Crew.
• Beer, wine and other
alcoholic beverages
must only be stored in
the designated storage
areas in Cottage Wine
and Craft 8eer.

1- Fintech (Slide Layer)
Your store Is required to pay for alcohol purchase orders upon
delivery. Fintech is a service that provides us a secure way to
pay for these deliveries electronically without having to worry
about having cash or checks on hand at the time of delivery.
Because we are paying upon delivery, It means that we cannot
receive a credit or refund on items that were found to not have
been delivered at a later time. Any discrepancies found at a
later date will be cycle counted at the store level and have an
impact on the store’s All In Shrink.

Non-Cottage Products?
• This may rarely occur, but you should know that stores CANNOT
receive Cottage products and non-Cottage products on the
same P0.
• If the vendor has combined both types of products on one invoice,
you must choose to receive either the Cottage products or the non-
Cottage products. Make sure you make a note on the invoice.
Non-Cottage Products?
• This may rarely occur, but you should know that Fintech CANNOT
receive non-Cottage products.
• If the vendor has combined both Cottage and non-Cottage products
onto one invoice, you must either REFUSE the delivery or ONLY
receive the alcoholic product. Make sure you make a note on the
invoice.
• If a vendor is requesting to use Fintech to pay for only non-alcoholic
product, REFUSE the delivery.

6.3 GF Beer variety
How Our Products Are Unique
We work with local breweries to provide a locally-focused
selection of great craft beer, hard kombucha, and hard
seltzer.
In addition, we have the greatest selection of certified
gluten-free beer. Certified gluten-free means the beer is
produced in a certified gluten-free facility which prevents
cross-contamination.

6.4 Alcoholic Kombucha
A Note on Hard Kombucha
The Cottage carries hard
kombucha that contains 0.5%
alcohol or more. Hard kombucha
must be treated like beer, and
customers are legally required to
be at least 21 years old to
purchase it.
You must be aware of the hard
kombucha brands your store
carries, which may include GT’s
and Wild Tonic, to ensure legal
compliance with ID checking.

The Honorable

City Council Members
2101 O’Neil Avenue
Cheyenne, Wyoming, 82001
January 21, 2022
Dear Mayor and Members of the City Council,
Please accept this letter as our introduction and cover letter for our application for a retail
liquor license for the City of Cheyenne. It has been a pleasure working with you all during the
rezoning, replatting and building portion of this project and we would like to thank you for your
assistance in that endeavor. As most of you know, Darin and I are renovating a 90+ year old
warehouse on the West Edge into a multi-use property consistent with the City’s West Edge Plan.
The warehouse, when completed, will have a bar with a very large outdoor patio and game space,
a package store or event space, a possible future brewery and a possible future grill area with a
pizza oven to provide a very limited menu and food options for the bar. We had hoped to have a
brewery on site at opening but our brewing equipment is a victim of the supply chain issue and is
still stuck overseas in the shipping process. We are also renovating the southwest corner of the
warehouse into an apartment to be used as employee housing. We are looking forward to
incorporating our outdoor space with the Reed Avenue Corridor Project and contributing to the
revitalization of the West Edge. If our application for a retail liquor license is granted, we will be
able to open sometime this spring.
We look forward to working with the City on this application and permit process and we
are happy to answer any questions or provide any additional information you may require.
Darin Westby

PROPOSED ALCOHOL TRAINING PROGRAM FOR WESTBY EDGE
Introductory statement:
We take this issue very seriously, we will provide consistent and substantive training for
all employees (and volunteers if any) serving alcohol in our establishment.
Initial orientation:
We will provide an initial orientation to all employees (and volunteers if any) when they
begin work about our commitment to a safe and secure establishment where alcohol will be served.
We will provide training options (see below) and detail requirements of updating training and
maintaining certifications.
Substantive training:
We will require that all employees (and volunteers if any) who sell, serve or deliver
alcoholic or malt beverages or provide on-site security are TIPS trained in a program approved by
the Wyoming Liquor Division by a certified trainer. This training will occur within 90 days of
employment of the initial date of providing services. We will maintain those records in our
business records and make sure that every person selling, serving, or delivering alcohol or involved
in on-site security is current on his/her TIPS training. This documentation will be available to law
enforcement personnel for inspection.
Alcohol Related Policies:
No alcohol will be sold or served to persons under the legal drinking age within the licensed
or permitted building or authorized areas of service.
All incidents requiring law enforcement responsive services will be immediately reported
by the licensee, permittee, employees, volunteers, on-site managers, agents or any persons
providing on-site security services. Immediate notice will be provided to the Police Department of
any criminal or illegal activity observed on the licensee’s or permittee’s property or adjoining
property affiliated with the sale or consumption of alcoholic liquor and malt beverages, including
the reporting of any assault, aggravated assault, or assault and battery.

WESTBY EDGE RESPONSES TO THE
LIQUOR LICENSE GENERAL GUIDELINES
For use by the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne in reviewing applications submitted
for one (1) additional retail liquor license available through the City of Cheyenne due to the
2020 revised population census.
A.
Pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 12-4-104 (b), a license shall not be issued ifthe licensing authority
finds from evidence presented at the public hearing:
1.
The welfare of the people residing in the vicinity of the proposed premises to be
licensed shall be adversely affected;
Westby Edge Brewing is located in a previously abandoned and run down
building in an area generally zoned Light Industrial (we rezoned our property
to Mixed Use Business in 2020 and it is now subject to the overlay). The
closest homes are a block away and will not be adversely affected in any way
by our business.
2.
The purpose of Wyoming Statutes, Title 12, Alcoholic Beverages, shall not be carried
out by the issuance of the license;
•
The purpose of Wyoming Statute Title 12 will be carried out by the issuance
of a license to Westby Edge.
3.
The number, type and location ofexisting licenses meet the needs ofthe vicinity under
consideration;
•
The Reed Avenue Corridor does not currently have any liquor licenses and the
closest one is the Rauzi license a few blocks away however they have
indicated that their facility will be an event space and not a bar or restaurant.
There are no bars or restaurants along the Reed avenue corridor or in the West
Edge.
4.
The desires ofthe residents ofthe City will not be met or satisfied by the issuance of
the license; or
•
Cheyenne residents have voted twice to fund the Reed Avenue Corridor
project on the West Edge with the vision of bars, restaurants and other
gathering spaces to bring people to the area.
5.
Any other reasonable restrictions or standards which may be imposed by the licensing
authority shall not be carried out by the issuance of the license.
•NIA
Pagelof2

B. Other considerations which may be considered include:
1.
Is the proposed premises to be licensed serving as an amenity compatible with other
neighborhood activities.
Westby Edge is directly on the Reed Avenue Corridor, a project that envisions
bars, restaurants and gathering spaces for Cheyenne. Not only would a license
for our business be compatible with the neighborhood activies, it would serve
as the anchor business for the West Edge and the Reed Avenue Corridor. We
have a capacity upwards of 350 people and the space to have a significant
liquor store, large bar and grill and catering ability. Although we have decided
to offer a limited menu of bar foods as a measure of responsibility since we
will be serving alcohol, we will not be able to meet the 60/40 split to make a
bar and grill license work. Our business will be a volume business and our
food will only provide an estimated 15-20% of our sales. We are also planning
on brewing our own beer but we will only have a small system and we will not
be able to manufacture enough beer to address the 60/40 split. Although some
establishments like the Paramount have been able to fit within the 60/40 split
based on the extended hours of operation of the coffee shop vs limited hours of
operation for the cocktail bar, our business model will not be successful if we
are forced to comply with the 60/40 split.
2.
Does the proposed site meet current zoning requirements.
•
Yes, it is currently zoned MUB with a zoning overlay allowing for multiple types
of uses.
3.
Would a license, if issued, help in economic development of a blighted property.
•
Our building is a 92 year old warehouse and original Taco Johns International
Headquarters. Prior to our purchase, the plan was to tear down this significant
building in Cheyenne history. The indoor facility is approximately 7000 sq/ft with
a similarly sized outdoor area for seating and games. We hope to make our facility
the anchor business, at least for the time being, to draw people to the Reed
Avenue Corridor and the West Edge. We hope that a thriving business will attract
other businesses to the area to begin to realize the City’s vision of a revitalized
West Edge and Reed Avenue Rail Corridor.
4.
Would a license, if issued, provide social and economic opportunities benefitting the
community.
•
A liquor license would allow us to operate a thriving business with large and
open indoor and outdoor facilities. Westby Edge will be a great place for
individuals, groups and families to gather, eat and drink, enjoy locally brewed
beer, watch games and other televised events and play games in the outdoor
Page2of2

area. We hope to employ a full time supervisor/manager and 12-15 part time
employees as well as hiring local accountants and others to assist in operating
the business. We plan to offer employees competitive salaries, health insurance
contributions and other benefits and profit sharing.
5.
Is the proposed premises to be licensed located within 500 feet of a hospital, church,
school or daycare facility.
•
It is not.
6.
Anticipated time frame in which the applicant plans to be operational and open to the
public.
•
We are currently finishing the south third of the property and finalizing the
mechanical/plumbing plan for the north 2/3rds. We are anticipating an opening
date of May or June of this year and we may be able to open a liquor store
sooner if we are given the license.
C. Consideration of supporting documents that may be submitted with the application by the
applicant, including, but not limited toa:
1.
Business Plan
(For example, information such as the following: whether the license will be used
to attract other businesses, renovate an historic building or building of community
significance, create a business anchor, anticipated business hours, customer offerings,
building design, the need or market segment to be filled by issuance of the license, possible
effects on existing businesses in and out of the area, anticipated number of’ employees to be
hired. along with the average wage and total annual payroll, and any franchise or chain
affiliation.)
•
Attached
2.
Financial Plan
(For example, information reflecting the applicant’s resources to acquire, construct
or remodel a building, if applicable, to be used as the licensed premises. A financial
statement must be submitted with each application; a Financial Plan, if submitted, would
be a more detailed description indicating the applicant’s ability to become operational to
provide the types(s) of services proposed under the license in a timely manner.)
•
Attached

BUSINESS AND FINANCIAL PLAN
Microbrewery/Bar &Grill: Westby Edge Brewing
Company Name, Address and Telephone Number:
Westby Edge LLC
714 W. 20th Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001
(307)421-4899
(307)421-4603
westbyedge@gmail.com
Westbyedge.com
Names, Job Titles, Contact Information of Owners:
Darin Westby, Owner, (307)421-4899
Misha Westby, Owner, (307)421-4603
Westby Edge Brewing will be a microbrewery located in Cheyenne, Wyoming on the West Edge.
Executive Summary
Although the domestic beer industry is dominated by mass producers, microbreweries are
thriving and have become destination spots in surrounding comunities. A microbrewery
produces a limited amount ofbeer, typically less than 15,000 U.S. barrels per year and generally
sell their products in house or distribute locally.
Westby Edge Brewing (WEB) will be a microbrewery/bar & grill located on the West Edge in
Cheyenne Wyoming. The property is owned by Darin and Misha Westby and was rezoned in
2020 from Industrial to Mixed Use Business. This business plan covers the startup phase of the
Westby
Edge
Brewing
Business/Financial
Plan
Page
1115

business and a brief outline for future growth.
BBL stands for “barrels.” A barrel of beer is 31 gallons and the standard size for a keg is a half
barrel. WEB has a 7 BBL brewing system on order that will be installed by March 2022. WEB
plans to start production at 724 barrels per year to be expanded to 1,500 by the end of year
five. WEB will produce its beer with the 7 BBL system operating at two brews a week.
Initially WEB will produce 80 percent ales and 20 percent lagers. The beer will be stored in
finishing tanks in the cooler that will be tapped through the wall and served directly from the
finishing tanks. When product needs to be transferred, the last BBL may be put into kegs
which will also be stored in the cooler.
We will also offer product for sale in the form of 32 ounce crowlers. A crowler is essentially a
growler in a can. The cans will be purchased from a distributor with our logo and canned
behind the bar.
We will offer six beers on tap with plans to expand to 12 by the end of year five.
Long-term goals include expanding capacity to 1,500 barrels (BBL) per year and expanding our
beers to include seasonal and specialty beers.
Although the success of microbreweries relies in large part on other microbreweries and creating
a microbrewery destination, WEB plans to embrace the microbrewery business model while also
setting itself apart by offering a wider variety of drinks and food. WEB will have a large cooler
directly behind the bar to allow for all beverages to be served on tap, this will include domestic
beer, wine and champagne or sparkling wine. WEB believes this will give it a competitive edge
since microbrewery beers are not for everyone. WEB will also offer liquor and a limited cocktail
menu.
In addition, in an effort to be responsible purveyors of alcoholic beverages, we are obtaining a
bar & grill license and plan to serve a limited menu using primarily two small commercial pizza
ovens. We anticipate that our food sales will be only 10-20 percent of our gross sales.
Microbreweries have a competitive advantage because of the uniqueness of the craft beverages
and the use of local and sustainable products. Microbreweries increase their market share by
Westby
Edge
Brewing
Business/Financial
Plan
Page
2115

building their brand and incorporating their business into the daily awareness of their
consumers.
Our marketing strategy will be to build awareness of our products and continually innovate our
brands and product lines. The strategy will be grounded in building our brand and establishing
WEB as a premier craft brewer and meeting/gathering space that caters to all tastes and the entire
community.
Owner investment, ownership of the property and land, a generous DDA grant, and a business
loan from FirsTier Bank have given WEB a significant cash infusion to renovate the structure,
landscape the outdoor space and begin production.
Sales projections are based on research conducted of local breweries and bar & grills in the
Cheyenne and surrounding areas. Projections place WEB’s gross sales revenues in excess of
$500,000 by the end of year three.
Objectives
WEB’s objectives for the first five years of operations include the following:
•
To create a gathering spot for the community that is welcoming and entices people to the
West Edge
•
To provide a location to watch a game, meet friends, have a great beer and a variety of
other alcoholic and non-alcoholic beverages and a limited food menu
•
Establish and maintain the highest quality by utilizing quality ingredients and hiring
motivated staff
•
Create profitability by carefully monitoring the costs of ingredients, appropriately pricing
menu items and establishing a solid customer base.
•
Continuously offer new beers, seltzer and products to maintain interest
Company Summary
We moved to Cheyenne in 1994 and we both believe in community involvement and have
volunteered for various organizations and charities while raising our family and working full
Wesiby
Edge
Brewing
Business/Financial
Plan
Page 3115

time. Darin serves on the Governor’s Cabinet as the Director of State Parks and Cultural
Resources and Misha is a Deputy Attorney General. We have raised three amazing children
in Cheyenne, Amanda Escobedo, Colton Westby and Brennan Westby who all remain in
Cheyenne. Of our community involvement, Frontier Days is the most significant with Darin
beginning his volunteer work in 1994. Darin served as the Concessions Committee Chairman,
the General Chairman and the Chairman of the Board until his term expired last year. With
our service to Frontier Days coming to an end, we decided we needed a new project and fell
in love with the Reed Avenue Corridor project and the West Edge. We sold our beautiful
home in Mustang Ridge and put all of our resources toward renovating a 90 year old
warehouse on Reed Avenue. We purchased the property in February of 2020 and began the
processing of rezoning and replatting the halfcity block. Rezoning was completed in late 2020
and the property is zoned as Mixed Use Business. We began the business plan for the brewery
in 2019 and although we have made some modifications, the basic idea of an anchor business
for the West Edge has been consistent throughout the planning.
During this pre-startup and renovation phase WEB accomplished the following:
•
Rezoned from Light Industrial to Mixed Use Business
•
Replatted the property into 5 parcels
•
Worked with architects and tradesman to put together building plans
•
Obtained a building permit
•
Created the business name and purchased the rights to the name through the Secretary
of State
•
Incorporated Westby Edge LLC as a Wyoming company

4115

Our company name is intended to promote the West End as well as serving as a family named
business. Our goal is to renovate a dilapidated but historically significant building into a
welcoming brewery/bar & grill. The warehouse started out as a lumber yard in the early 1920s.
It was then repurposed by the Holmes family into an RV and boat manufacturing facility. The
facility was utilized to make the first Taco Johns Taco trucks and then ultimately served as the
first Taco Johns International Headquarters. The warehouse fell into disrepair and we have
spent the last two years cleaning and repairing the building and we are now in the
construction phase with an anticipated opening date of late spring early summer 2022.
The projections from the 2021 Census estimate the population of Cheyenne at 65,035. There
are currently 4 other breweries in the downtown/West Edge and the DDA has started
marketing Cheyenne as a brewery destination. WEB would like to continue the brewery
culture in Cheyenne and cross-market with the other local breweries.
Company Ownership
WEB is a Limited Liability Company owned by its members Darin and Melissa (Misha) Westby.
They have equal ownership in the company with shared management duties and ultimate
decision making authority.
Company Location
WEB encompasses a one half city block boarded by Reed Avenue to the West, 20th Street to
the South and Bent Avenue to the East. The Brewery/Bar & Grill is on the South East half of
the lot. The warehouse is 10,300 sq/ft with a 1,900 sq/ft two bedroom apartment on the South
East corner, an event space/over flow area of potential liquor store in the remaining South
third of the building and a loft space for offices and potential rentable space. The entire north
two-thirds of the building is the main brewery/bar & grill with a seating capacity of 250+ and
a large outdoor patio space for seating and games.
Westby
Edge
Brewing
Business/Financial
Plan
Page 5115

The location has the following advantages:
•
It is in the middle of the planned Reed Avenue Corridor Project
•
There is a large outdoor space for seating and games
•
There is parking available in the city lot across 20th Street, on street parking around the
building and around the park and potential after hours parking across Reed Avenue
•
The renovation of the warehouse does not displace any residents and there are no
residents or businesses close enough to be bothered by music or noise from the
establishment.
•
It is within walking distance of other breweries, restaurants and retail businesses making
it a tourist destination
•
The warehouse has a barn structure with a 30+ foot ceiling and open beam structure
•
The use of 2 half glass garage doors and a full glass garage door with open up the space
for an indoor/outdoor feeling during warm weather
•
The size of the main area allows for a 47 foot long bar, room for multiple 8 person and 4
person tables and a space for live music and dancing
•
The metal support structure provides the perfect setting for unique seating including
swing seats around the secondary outdoor bars (half glass garage doors)
•
Most importantly, WEB will provide an anchor for the West Edge and hopefully
encourage other businesses along the corridor

Products
WEB plans to start with 4 beers and 1 seltzer and expand from there.
Lagers:
Mexican Lager
— a light easy drinking lager reminiscent of a Corona or Modelo
Mexican Amber Lager
— a light amber similar to a Negra Modelo
Ales;
Standard IPA
— we will model our IPA on Odell’s IPA using locally grown hops for a distinctive
flavor
Hazy IPA
— this will be a milder IPA, still hop forward but not as high IBU
Seltzer:
Our seltzer will be a plain seltzer like a hard club soda and we will provide flavor syrups to make
the seltzer flavor of the customer’s choice.
WEB also plans to partner with local home brewers who would like to scale up a recipe for
commercial consumption.
WEB
will
offer
a limited liquor/cocktail menu
as well
as domestic beer, wine and
champagne/sparkling wine on tap.
WEB will offer a limited menu
— see sample attached.
The brewery/bar & grill will be open from 11 a.m. to 10 p.m. Sunday through Thursday and
11:00 a.m. to 12 p.m. Friday and Saturday.
Alcohol Sales
WEB will sell all beers made on premises based on the cost of ingredients and prices will range
from $5.50 to $6.75 per 16 oz beer. We will also sell flights ofbeer ranging from $8.25 to $9.50.
Westby
Edge
Brewing Business/Financial Plan
Page 8115

We will sell domestic beer for $4.50 and wine and champagne for $6.50. Our cocktails will
range from $6.00 to $7.50.
Production
In our first year of operation WEB will produce 724 barrels per year: 40 percent (290 bbl)
Lagers and 40 percent (290 bbl) Ales and 10 percent (72 BBL) seltzers.
Production will take place 52 weeks/year as follows:
•
Lagers
— 45 cycles/fermenter/year (52 brewing
weeks/2 week fermentation)
•
Ales
— 45 cycles/fermenter/year
(52 brewing
weeks/3-4 week fermentation)
•
Seltzer
- 12 cycles/fermenter/year (52 brewing
weeks/1-2 week fermentation)
Our current system is a 7 BBL 2-vessel system with 4 x 7 BBL fermenters and 4 x 7 BBL bright
tanks. The brewhouse turns crushed grain, water, and hops into wort. From there the wort
temperature drops and it is moved to fermenters, and yeast added to turn the wort to beer.
We will have 2 hot on demand water tanks to provide hot water and the kettle will provide
the boiled water
In order to allow for future expansion, we will purchase 2 more 15 BBL fermenters to allow
for double batches.
Market Analysis Summary
According to the Brewers Association, there were just under 2,800 microbreweries in the U.S.
at the end of 2019. The rate of growth from 2018 to 2019 was 21.8 percent.
During the last five years, hundreds of microbreweries came online while the number of
annual closings ranged between 30 and 40 each year.
Special brewery permits and inexpensive federal taxes make microbreweries a generally
profitable undertaking.
Westby
Edge
Brewing
Business/Financial
Plan
Page
9115

One reason microbreweries continue to succeed is because of their regulatory environment.
Large brewers (brewers that sell over 60,000 barrels yearly) must pay $18 per barrel in excise
tax, while small breweries only pay $16 per barrel.
Microbreweries have become a tourist draw and the front range has a multitude of brewery
destinations including Cheyenne. The lifestyle index for alcoholic beverages is highest for
microbrews, which suggests that more people drink craft beer than any other alcoholic
beverage. The county population is 184,000 and it is estimated that 28,681 of those people
drink microbrew beer.
Market Segmentation
WEB will be a brewery on the West Edge directly on the Reed Avenue Rail Corridor. WEB will
be in a purchased property with a total mortgage payment of less than $7,000. The Reed Avenue
Rail Corridor will provide a walking business base for the facility.
WEB is one and a half blocks from Black Tooth Brewery and within a 1.5 mile radius of the
other breweries in Cheyenne. The addition of a brewery will bring more people to the West
Edge and make Cheyenne a destination spot for microbrewery enthusiasts.
While it is our belief that a “rising tide floats all boats” we are also cognizant of the market
share issue with a finite number of patrons. WEB will be unique in the Cheyenne
microbrewery community because of our ability to serve liquor, domestic beer, wine and
champagne in addition to a small food menu.
According to Visit Cheyenne, 1.1 million overnight visitors came to Cheyenne and Laramie
County in 2019. In addition to local consumers, WEB can also benefit from these out oftown
visitors. In addition, these visitors spent $380 million in the Cheyenne area providing a solid
base of out of town revenue.
Cheyenne has 27,647 people in the labor force with an unemployment rate of 5.1. of those,
1,927 or 7.9 are employed in the food service/entertainment industry. The average household
income in 2019 was $64,000 and Forbes has a projected annual job growth for Wyoming of
0.2%. Although the economy has suffered during the pandemic, the rate of spending at bars

and restaurants including breweries has increased from 2019.
Marketing Plan
The WEB marketing plan will be focused on creating a brand identity that the public will
associate with a fun environment and quality food and drink. Our goal will be to appeal to all
audiences from families to individuals and microbrew enthusiasts to domestic beer and wine
drinkers.
Our marketing efforts will include the following strategies:
•
Print advertising: including marketing materials aimed at tourists
•
Web site: information on our company, our beers, drinks and food
•
Promotional items: hats, tees and other merchandise without logo
•
Word ofmouth: we plan to have several soft openings and tasting parties prior to opening
to spread the word about WEB
WEB has completed the design of a signature trademark to be used in our marketing materials
and on labels for our products.
Tactical Objectives
HBC has identified the following tactical objectives for future activities that can be tied to specific
marketing strategies, tracked and managed.
•
Establish a varied customer base
•
Establish a reputation for quality food and beverage
•
Achieve a profitable return on investment within 3 years
•
Establish and maintain a cash flow sufficient to pay all salaries, costs of business and
provide a fund to put back into the business for improvements.
Competitive Edge
The craft beer market has experienced explosive growth that is predicted to continue into the
future. Because craft beers tend to be marketed to a select geographic area it is possible to build a
Westby
Edge

loyal following based on familiarity.
WEB has competitive advantage because of several factors:
•
Community involvement and connections of the owners
•
Family expertise in the brewing industry
•
Ownership of the building
•
Location on a developing walking corridor
Moreover, WEB as a family business can utilize the skills and education of family members to
help the business thrive.
Management Summary
WEB will be led by its owners and by the master brewer who all have the same philosophy:
bringing people to the West Edge to enjoy a fun and entertaining environment and partake in
quality food and beverages.
Darin
Westby—Owner/member:
responsible
for
operations.
Darin
has
contributed
significantly to the Cheyenne community from his volunteer work with Frontier Days to
coaching his children in many sports including softball, basketball and hockey. Darin is a
microbrew enthusiast and has researched microbreweries for years.
Misha Westby—Owner/member: Misha will be responsible for operations, marketing and
assistant brewer. Misha has also worked and volunteered in Cheyenne and throughout the
community for 25 years. She trained with her daughter to become certified and will serve as
the assistant brewer.
Brennan Westby—Master Brewer: Brennan has her degree in biology with an emphasis on
chemistry from the University of Wyoming. She spent the last two years interning and
achieving the required 100 hours of interning for a master brewer. She recently completed a
certification course and obtained her master brewers certification. Brennan will be
responsible for brewery operations.
Additional future hires:
Manager/supervisor: to supervise the business side of the project and manage personnel

Personnel Plan
In addition to owners Darin and Misha Westby and master brewer Brennan Westby, we will
plan to hire a manager/supervisor who will oversee two part time shifts. We plan to hire
between 32 and 35 part time servers and bartenders. The shifts will be 10:30 to 11:00a.m. to
4:00 p.m. and 4:00 to closing. We will pay competitive wages, provide a contribution towards
health insurance and other benefits as well as profit sharing. We will offer a salary of $48,000
plus
an
apartment
to
our
brewer and
a
salary
of
$40,000
to
$45,000
for
the
manager/supervisor. We plan to start servers and bartenders out at 6-8 dollars an hour before
tips and kitchen staff slightly higher. We will have shared tip system so that all staff receive
some tips with the servers and bartenders receiving a higher percentage. We will also pay
bonuses on a quarterly basis based on a profit calculation system.
